# mlx-rs

## Summary

mlx-rs is a Rust-language binding and high-level wrapper around Apple's MLX machine learning framework, designed to run on Apple Silicon hardware using Metal GPU acceleration. The repository is organized as a multi-crate Cargo workspace with the following principal components: (1) a core tensor and operations library (pmetal-mlx-rs) that exposes MLX primitives such as tensor creation, arithmetic, linear algebra, activation functions, normalization, dropout, loss functions, and fast-path operations including RoPE, layer normalization, scaled dot-product attention, and custom Metal kernels via MSL shaders; (2) a language model library (mlx-lm) that builds transformer-style LLM inference pipelines supporting architectures such as Mistral and Qwen3, including KV-cache management and autoregressive sampling; (3) procedural macro helpers that reduce boilerplate for defining neural network modules; and (4) runnable example binaries covering MNIST training and LLM text generation. Internally, mlx-lm and the examples depend on the core pmetal-mlx-rs library. Externally, a separate repository named 'inference' is observed to call mlx-rs extensively (many hundreds of call edges), indicating that mlx-rs serves as a foundational inference engine library for at least one downstream consumer. The repository itself has no Rust-ecosystem upstream dependencies beyond Apple's MLX C/C++ library (accessed via FFI) and standard utility crates. The overall architecture follows a layered pattern: low-level FFI bindings → safe Rust tensor abstractions → neural network module system → model-specific implementations → executable examples and downstream consumers.

## Projects

### mlx-rs

mlx-rs is a Rust binding and high-level wrapper around Apple's MLX machine learning framework, targeting Metal GPU acceleration on Apple Silicon. The project provides core tensor operations, neural network layers (activations, dropout, normalization), fast primitives (RoPE, layer norm, scaled dot-product attention, custom Metal kernels), loss functions, and example applications including MNIST training and Mistral/Qwen3 LLM inference. It is structured as a multi-crate workspace with a core library (pmetal-mlx-rs), a language model library (mlx-lm), procedural macro helpers, and runnable examples.
